  # Project Initiation & Development Instructions                               
                                                                                
  You are acting as my **Product Architect, Business Analyst, UX/UI Designer,   
  Solution Architect, and Senior Software Engineer**.                           
                                                                                
  Your responsibility is to guide the complete development of a                 
  production-quality application, starting from understanding the business      
  requirements and ending with deployment and maintenance.                      
                                                                                
  This project must be approached systematically. Do not rush into coding.      
                                                                                
  ## 1. First: Understand the Existing Documentation                            
                                                                                
  I will provide you with:                                                      
                                                                                
  1. A PDF containing the application's documentation, requirements, workflows, 
  and functional descriptions.                                                  
  2. An image containing the colour palette that I want to use for both Light   
  and Dark themes.                                                              
                                                                                
  **Before making ANY decisions or proposing implementation:**                  
                                                                                
  - Read the entire PDF, not just selected sections.                            
  - Understand the application's purpose, business logic, modules, workflows,   
  users, and dependencies.                                                      
  - Study the provided colour palette carefully.                                
  - Understand how the light and dark colour systems should translate into the  
  application's UI.                                                             
  - Identify missing information, contradictions, ambiguities, and incomplete   
  requirements.                                                                 
  - Do not assume that something works a particular way unless the              
  documentation confirms it.                                                    
  - Do not invent business requirements or workflows.                           
  - Do not make architectural or technical decisions without sufficient         
  understanding.                                                                
                                                                                
  Start by explaining your understanding of the application in simple language. 
                                                                                
  I want to understand:                                                         
  - What exactly are we building?                                               
  - What problem does it solve?                                                 
  - Who will use it?                                                            
  - What are its main modules?                                                  
  - How will the application work?                                              
  - What are the major user journeys?                                           
  - What happens behind the scenes?                                             
  - What information needs to be stored?                                        
  - What integrations or external dependencies are involved?                    
                                                                                
  Separate confirmed information from assumptions and unanswered questions.     
                                                                                
  ## 2. Requirement Clarification — Start With Questions                        
                                                                                
  After analysing the documentation and colour palette, your FIRST deliverable  
  must be a structured questionnaire.                                           
                                                                                
  Do not start UX/UI design, database design, architecture, or development      
  before clarifying the requirements.                                           
                                                                                
  Ask me questions about anything that is unclear or necessary to build the     
  application correctly.                                                        
                                                                                
  Cover areas such as:                                                          
                                                                                
  - Business requirements and application purpose.                              
  - User roles and permissions.                                                 
  - Authentication and authorisation.                                           
                                                                                
  ──── (279 lines hidden) ───────────────────────────────────────────────────── 
  dencies.                                                                      
  - Prefer maintainable, secure, scalable solutions.                            
  - Follow established coding standards.                                        
  - Avoid unnecessary abstractions and overengineering.                         
  - Write readable, production-quality code.                                    
  - Do not use placeholder implementations in production functionality.         
  - Never claim something is implemented or tested unless it actually is.       
  - Run relevant tests after making changes.                                    
  - Keep documentation synchronised with implementation.                        
  - Do not overwrite existing work without inspecting it first.                 
  - Before modifying an existing feature, understand its current implementation 
  and dependencies.                                                             
                                                                                
  ### Development Workflow                                                      
                                                                                
  Before implementing any feature:                                              
                                                                                
  1. Understand the requirement.                                                
  2. Review related documentation.                                              
  3. Inspect the existing code.                                                 
  4. Identify affected components.                                              
  5. Explain the proposed implementation when it involves important decisions.  
  6. Obtain approval where required.                                            
  7. Implement the feature.                                                     
  8. Test it.                                                                   
  9. Update relevant documentation.                                             
  10. Update the decision log if a decision was made.                           
                                                                                
  ## 7. Collaboration Rules                                                     
                                                                                
  I want to participate in important product and technical decisions.           
                                                                                
  You should:                                                                   
  - Explain technical concepts in practical language.                           
  - Present recommendations with reasoning.                                     
  - Highlight trade-offs.                                                       
  - Distinguish facts from recommendations.                                     
  - Ask before making major decisions.                                          
  - Avoid unnecessary technical complexity.                                     
  - Keep me informed about what is completed and what remains.                  
  - Maintain continuity between development sessions.                           
                                                                                
  Do not assume that because I have mentioned a technology, every architectural 
  decision has already been made.                                               
                                                                                
  ## 8. Immediate First Task                                                    
                                                                                
  For now, DO NOT WRITE CODE.                                                   
                                                                                
  Do not start creating the application.                                        
                                                                                
  Do not design screens yet.                                                    
                                                                                
  Your immediate responsibilities are:                                          
                                                                                
  **Step 1:** Read the entire PDF documentation.                                
                                                                                
  **Step 2:** Analyse the provided light/dark colour palette image.             
                                                                                
  **Step 3:** Explain your understanding of the application, including its      
  purpose, modules, workflows, users, and how everything connects.              
                                                                                
  **Step 4:** Identify unclear requirements, contradictions, missing            
  information, and decisions that need my input.                                
                                                                                
  **Step 5:** Create a structured, documentation-specific questionnaire to      
  clarify these points.                                                         
                                                                                
  **Step 6:** Wait for my responses.                                            
                                                                                
  Once the requirements are clarified, we will proceed to UX research and UX/UI 
  design before moving to technical architecture and development.               
                                                                                
  **Remember: Understanding the product correctly is more important than        
  starting development quickly.**

⏺ I've read the whole PDF (3 pages) and both palette images, and written the
  project docs. No code, no installs.

  What we're building

  - Two Flutter apps, Admin and Staff, each for Android and iOS. Both use one
    ASP.NET Core API and one SQL Server database. There is no web portal (the
    PDF excludes it).
  - Problem it solves: tracking where each vehicle is parked, who ran it, for
    how long, and how full each lot is.
  - Admin journey: create categories and types → add lots with capacity → add
    vehicles (each gets a QR code automatically, which admins download and
    print) → add staff. Admins also move vehicles between lots and use a
    dashboard and reports for occupancy and hours by vehicle and by staff.
  - Staff journey: log in → scan a vehicle's QR → see its details and lot →
    Start (time recorded) → scan again → End (hours calculated).
  - Data stored: admins, staff, categories and types, vehicles with their QR
    codes, lots, start/end records, transfer history.
  - Integrations: only the camera (for QR) and saving/sharing files. The PDF
    doesn't mention email, SMS, push notifications or payments.

  Full version, with confirmed vs assumed marked: PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md

  Key findings

  - .NET 8 support ended on 10 Nov 2026, five days ago, so it gets no more
    security patches. I recommend .NET 10, the current long-term support release
    (supported to Nov 2028). This needs your approval.
  - This Mac has no development tools installed: no .NET SDK, Flutter, Xcode
    (only the command-line tools), Android SDK, Java or Docker. Your shell's
    PATH points to Flutter and Android folders that no longer exist.
  - The PDF has 11 gaps. For example, the Staff app shows an "assigned lot" but
    admins have no way to assign one, and the PDF allows deleting staff, which
    would break their hours history.
  - Palette: only Canary has an exact value (#FFE014). From the images I
    estimated Ink Heather ≈ #262A33 and Putty Grey ≈ #C1BBAF.
    - Canary on a light background has a contrast ratio of 1.24:1, so it can
      never be text or icons in the light theme. It works as a fill behind dark
      text (12.3:1).
    - Image 2 labels a beige swatch "Ink Heather", which contradicts image 1.

  Round 1 questions (★ = my recommendation)

  A. Business
  - A1. What are these vehicles, and what does "operating" one mean?
    - a) your own fleet or equipment, kept in your own yards
    - b) rental vehicles
    - c) customers' cars (valet or car park)
    - d) other
    - Only you can answer this one.
  - A2. One organisation, or several companies with separate data? ★ one
  - A3. How many vehicles, lots, staff and admins now and in about 3 years?
    Scans per day at peak? One timezone or several?
  - A4. Are billing, maintenance, fuel, GPS tracking and customer features out
    of scope for v1? ★ all out
  - A5. The PDF mentions "development stages" and "project assumptions" sections
    that aren't in it. Is there a fuller version?

  B. Roles
  - B1. Admin levels:
    - a) one admin role
    - b) Super Admin plus Admin ★
    - c) Super Admin plus admins limited to certain lots
  - B2. Lots assigned to staff:
    - a) one lot
    - b) several lots ★
    - c) the assignment is only for display
  - B3. Can one person be both admin and staff? ★ no, separate accounts
  - B4. Admins and time records:
    - a) view only
    - b) can force-end sessions and correct times, with a required reason that
      is logged ★
    - c) full manual entry

  C. Start/End (the core workflow)
  - C1. Does Start mean the vehicle leaves the lot (its space frees up), or does
    it work inside the lot (its space stays taken)? Only you can answer this
    one.
  - C2. Where is the vehicle after End?
    - a) always back in its own lot
    - b) staff pick the lot it was returned to, and a transfer is recorded
      automatically
    - c) it must return to its own lot
    - ★ a) if vehicles always come back to the same lot, otherwise b)
  - C3. Who can end a session?
    - a) only the person who started it
    - b) any staff member at that lot ★
    - c) anyone
    - Can one person run more than one vehicle at a time? ★ no
  - C4. A session nobody ends:
    - a) stays open, and the dashboard flags it after a set limit ★
    - b) closes automatically
  - C5. Record anything besides time (hour-meter or odometer reading, fuel,
    photo, notes, GPS, job reference)? ★ time only
  - C6. Statuses: Available, In Operation, Inactive. Add "Under Maintenance"?
    Add "In Transfer"?

  D. Lots and transfers
  - D1. Capacity:
    - a) one number per lot ★
    - b) numbered bays
    - c) capacity per vehicle type
    - When a lot is full: block, or warn? ★ warn
  - D2. Transfers:
    - a) admin updates the location instantly ★
    - b) admin creates the transfer, staff confirm on arrival
    - c) staff can start transfers too
    - Allowed while the vehicle is running? ★ no
  - D3. Lot details: ★ name, code, address, optional map pin. No map view.

  E. Vehicles and QR codes
  - E1. ★ Asset number required and unique. Plate, VIN, make, model, year and
    photo optional. No documents.
  - E2. Each type belongs to a category? ★ yes. Please send real examples.
  - E3. QR codes, all ★:
    - the QR holds a random code, not readable vehicle data
    - reissuing a QR deactivates the old one
    - download as both an image and a printable PDF label
    - staff can search by asset or plate number if a QR won't scan
    - the Admin app gets a scanner too
    - Do you need bulk printing?

  F. Sign-in
  - F1. ★ Staff use username or employee ID + password. Admins use email +
    password. SMS one-time codes are possible but need a paid provider.
  - F2. Forgotten password: ★ admins reset staff passwords, admins reset their
    own by email link, and new accounts must change the password at first login.
  - F3. Do staff use personal phones, or company phones shared between shifts?
  - F4. ★ Deactivate records instead of deleting them, so history is kept.

  G. Connectivity
  - G1. Is internet reliable at every lot?
    - a) online only ★ if yes
    - b) Start/End saved offline and synced later
    - c) the whole app works offline

  H. Tech
  - H1. Approve .NET 10? ★
  - H2. Will you develop on this Mac? Is there an existing SQL Server, or should
    it run locally in Docker? ★ install the tools on this Mac, only after your
    OK.

  I. Palette
  - I1. Do you have exact hex values for Ink Heather and Putty Grey? If not, ★
    I'll use my estimates.
  - I2. ★ Approve this reading:
    - Light theme: warm off-white background, Putty surfaces, Ink text, Canary
      accent
    - Dark theme: Ink Heather background, Putty text, Canary accent

  You can reply like A1: a, or accept ★ for B–I. Each question's reasoning is in
  OPEN_QUESTIONS.md. Round 2 covers reports and exports, notifications,
  hosting, app store distribution, branding and languages.

  Files

  - CLAUDE.md: your three instructions word for word, plus working principles
    and workflow
  - PROJECT_DECISIONS.md: decisions D-001 to D-005 (D-003 .NET 10 and D-004
    palette are proposed, not yet approved)
  - PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md
  - OPEN_QUESTIONS.md
  - docs/source/: copies of the PDF and both palette images, so they don't
    depend on Downloads or temp folders

  I can also turn the questionnaire into a shareable page if your client needs
  to answer it.

  Sources:
  - Microsoft .NET blog: .NET 8 and 9 end of support
    (https://devblogs.microsoft.com/dotnet/dotnet-8-9-end-of-support/)
  - .NET releases and support policy
    (https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/releases-and-support)
  - Flutter release archive (https://docs.flutter.dev/install/archive)
  - Flutter docs: what's new (https://docs.flutter.dev/release/whats-new.html)

⏺ I read all 31 Staff UI screens. They answer 10 of the form's questions fully
  or in part, and they add features the requirements PDF doesn't mention.

  Questions the Staff UI answers

  Q: A1
  Answer from the mockup: Municipality fleet ("DM" logo, "Municipality Parking
  Lot", Dubai locations). Mini trucks are driven by drivers from the lot to
  work locations
  Status: Guessed, please confirm
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Q: A3
  Answer from the mockup: Dubai, UAE: one country, one timezone (UTC+4). Vehicle

  and staff counts not shown
  Status: Partly answered
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Q: B2
  Answer from the mockup: The starting lot is already filled in when a trip
  starts, so each staff member has a home lot
  Status: Partly: one lot or several still open
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Q: C1
  Answer from the mockup: Start = the vehicle leaves the lot for a chosen work
  location. End = staff tap "Vehicle received"
  Status: Answered
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Q: C2
  Answer from the mockup: No choice of return lot at End
  Status: Guessed: same lot, please confirm
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Q: C5
  Answer from the mockup: Start records driver (QR or typed ID), vehicle (QR or
  typed ID), driver signature, starting lot and destination. End scans driver
  and vehicle, then shows a summary with zone and area. No meter reading, fuel
  or photo
  Status: Answered
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Q: C6
  Answer from the mockup: "Active" status is shown, and trips can be cancelled
  Status: Partly answered
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Q: E1
  Answer from the mockup: Vehicle no., name/model, plate number, image
  Status: Partly: which are required/unique still open
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Q: E3
  Answer from the mockup: Typing the ID works when a QR won't scan. Drivers have

  QR codes too
  Status: Partly answered
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Q: F1
  Answer from the mockup: Staff sign in with User ID + password and a "Remember
  me" box. Admins use the same screen
  Status: Answered for staff; guessed for admins

  New things the mockup adds, not in the requirements PDF
  - Drivers: a separate kind of person with their own QR code, photo, licence
    number and so on.
  - Work locations: destinations such as Dubai Beach and Dubai Park, each with a
    zone and area.
  - Change a running trip: swap the vehicle or the driver ("Start again").
  - Cancel a trip: with a reason (driver, vehicle, technical or other issue) and
    a note.
  - Attendance: check in, check out, lunch and tea break, each with a photo and
    GPS location; a schedule shows present, absent and late counts.
  - Staff Report tile: on the home screen, but no report screen is designed.
  - One login screen with a Staff/Admin switch: this conflicts with the PDF's
    two separate apps.

  The mockup's sample numbers contradict each other (10:20 to 12:20 is shown as
  both "03:20:12" and "2 h 20 min"), so I'm treating them as placeholder data.

  Remaining questions: they're in the new form above. It has:
  - 11 new questions (N1–N11) raised by the mockup
  - 3 to confirm my guesses (A1, C2, F1b)
  - the unanswered Round 1 questions

  C1, C5 and the staff sign-in question are gone because the mockup answers
  them. Old C3b (one active trip per person) is now N11, covering both drivers
  and vehicles.

  I saved the findings in OPEN_QUESTIONS.md and PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md, and the
  mockup PDF in docs/source/. Nothing has been recorded as a decision yet; that
  happens when you submit the form.

❯ # Vehicle Management Application – Functional Requirements & Clarifications   
                                                                                
  ### 1. Staff Assignment and Responsibilities                                  
                                                                                
  The Admin will create and manage all Staff accounts.                          
                                                                                
  Each staff member will have a dedicated profile. The staff member's           
  responsibilities and access will be limited to the following:                 
                                                                                
  - View their own profile.                                                     
  - View details of the vehicles they have started.                             
  - View the Parking Lot details from which a vehicle was started.              
  - View available parking spaces across all parking lots.                      
  - Start a vehicle when taking it out of the parking lot.                      
  - End the vehicle session when returning it to the parking lot.               
                                                                                
  Once the staff member ends a vehicle session, the application should          
  automatically display the complete session details, including:                
                                                                                
  - Vehicle details                                                             
  - Vehicle category                                                            
  - Parking Lot from which the vehicle was started                              
  - Start date and time                                                         
  - End date and time                                                           
  - Total duration the vehicle remained outside the parking lot                 
                                                                                
  ### 2. Handling Active Vehicles and Missed End Entries                        
                                                                                
  The Admin should be able to see all currently active vehicles in real time.   
                                                                                
  - The Admin can manually end any active vehicle session.                      
  - Staff members are also responsible for ending their respective vehicle      
  sessions when returning vehicles.                                             
  - If a staff member forgets to end a vehicle session, the application should  
  continuously send reminders or notifications.                                 
  - An active timer should remain visible in the top navigation bar of the      
  Staff application from the moment the vehicle is started until the session is 
  ended.                                                                        
  - The timer should continue running until the vehicle is marked as Ended by   
  either the Staff or Admin.                                                    
                                                                                
  This will help ensure that no vehicle remains active in the system without    
  proper tracking.                                                              
                                                                                
  ### 3. Vehicle Start and End Details                                          
                                                                                
  Whenever a vehicle is started or ended, the application should record all     
  necessary information.                                                        
                                                                                
  **Vehicle Start Details:**                                                    
  - Staff Member Name and ID                                                    
  - Driver Name                                                                 
  - Vehicle Registration Number                                                 
  - Vehicle Category                                                            
  - Vehicle Brand and Model                                                     
  - Parking Lot Name from which the vehicle was started                         
  - Start Date and Time                                                         
                                                                                
  **Vehicle End Details:**                                                      
  - Staff Member Name and ID                                                    
  - Driver Name                                                                 
  - Vehicle Details                                                             
  - Original Parking Lot Details                                                
  - Start Date and Time                                                         
  - End Date and Time                                                           
  - Total Duration the Vehicle Remained Outside                                 
                                                                                
  All records should be maintained in the system for future reference and       
  reporting.                                                                    
                                                                                
  ### 4. Parking Lot Capacity and Configuration                                 
                                                                                
  The Admin should have the option to add and manage multiple Parking Lots.     
                                                                                
  While adding a new Parking Lot, the Admin should be able to configure all     
  necessary details, including:                                                 
                                                                                
  - Parking Lot Type (Open Parking / Underground Parking / Other)               
  - Total Parking Capacity                                                      
  - Vehicle Type-wise Capacity                                                  
  - Available Parking Spaces                                                    
  - Parking Lot Status (Active / Inactive)                                      
                                                                                
  The system should automatically calculate the occupied and available parking  
  spaces based on vehicle entry and exit records.                               
                                                                                
  The Admin should have complete control over Parking Lot configuration and     
  capacity management.                                                          
                                                                                
  ### 5. Parking Lot Details                                                    
                                                                                
  The following information should be available while creating or managing a    
  Parking Lot:                                                                  
                                                                                
  1. Parking Lot Name                                                           
  2. Parking Lot Code / Unique ID                                               
  3. Full Address                                                               
  4. Google Maps Location Pin                                                   
  5. Contact Person Name                                                        
  6. Contact Number                                                             
  7. Operating Hours (Opening and Closing Time)                                 
  8. Vehicle Type-wise Capacity                                                 
  9. Total Parking Capacity                                                     
  10. Parking Lot Status (Active / Inactive)                                    
                                                                                
  The application should also provide a Map View to display all registered      
  Parking Lot locations.                                                        
                                                                                
  ### 6. Vehicle Details                                                        
                                                                                
  The Admin should be able to add and manage vehicle information with the       
  following details:                                                            
                                                                                
  1. Vehicle Registration Number (Number Plate)                                 
  2. Vehicle Type (Car, Garbage Truck, Ambulance, etc.)                         
  3. Vehicle Brand                                                              
  4. Vehicle Model                                                              
  5. Vehicle Color                                                              
  6. Vehicle Photo                                                              
                                                                                
  These details should be available whenever a vehicle is started or ended.     
                                                                                
  ### 7. Admin and Staff Login System                                           
                                                                                
  **Admin Login:**                                                              
  - A dedicated Admin account will be created.                                  
  - The Admin username/ID and password will be configured and provided by us.   
  - The Admin will have complete access to manage the application, including    
  Parking Lots, Staff, Vehicles, and Reports.                                   
                                                                                
  **Staff Login:**                                                              
  - The Admin will create individual accounts for each Staff member.            
  - Every Staff member will receive a unique Staff ID and password from the     
  Admin.                                                                        
  - Each Staff ID will be unique and used for login.                            
                                                                                
  **Example Staff ID:** `DM324DU2131`                                           
                                                                                
  Staff members will log in using their assigned unique Staff ID and password.  
                                                                                
  Each staff member will have access only to the permitted Staff                
  functionalities, while the Admin will have complete control over the          
  application.                                                                  
                                                                                
  ---                                                                           
                                                                                
  **Note:** All vehicle activities, parking records, staff activities, and      
  start/end timestamps should be stored systematically to maintain accurate     
  records and provide complete tracking and reporting functionality.            
  this is a response for some of question i asked client does it answer your    
  questions as previous list all question of your that this answer and ask      
  those question again that this above reponse doesnt answer

  │      │ status. Plus a map showing all lots                              │
  ├──────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ E1   │ Vehicle details: registration number, type, brand, model,        │
  │      │ colour, photo                                                    │
  ├──────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ F1 / │ Staff log in with a unique Staff ID (e.g. DM324DU2131) and a     │
  │  F1b │ password from the admin. Admin login details are set up by your  │
  │      │ team                                                             │
  ├──────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │      │ Staff can only see their profile, the vehicles they started, lot │
  │ N9   │  details and free spaces in all lots, plus start and end trips.  │
  │      │ No other reports                                                 │
  └──────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Partly answered: A2 (one admin account suggests a single organisation), B4
  (the admin can end a trip, but can they correct its times?), F2 (the admin
  issues staff passwords, but there's no forgotten-password flow).

  New questions the reply raises
  - K1: "Available spaces" is listed both as something the admin sets and as
    calculated automatically. Which is it?
  - K2: When do reminders start, how often do they repeat, and should the admin
    be alerted too?
  - K3: Do lot operating hours only show as information, or block starts and
    ends outside them?
  - K4: Is the Staff ID generated by the system or typed in by the admin? What
    does its pattern mean?
  - K5: Only the one admin account, or can more be added?
  - K6: The client's staff list leaves out the mockup's attendance, change trip,
    cancel trip, driver signature, destination and driver QR features. Which
    stay?

  Conflicts to settle: "Driver Name" is the only mention of drivers, with no
  driver management (N2). The client says "category" in one place and "type" in
  another (E2). A single top-bar timer suggests one vehicle out per staff member
  at a time (N11).

  What the reply adds to the build: push notifications for reminders, a Google
  Maps API key and billing account (whose account is a Round 2 question), and
  live updates for the admin's active-vehicle list.

  All remaining questions are in the form above. The ones marked "for you" (N1,
  H1, H2, I1, I2) are your decisions, not the client's; the rest can go to the
  client. I saved their reply in
  docs/source/client-clarifications-2026-11-18.md, recorded it as decision
  D-006, and updated OPEN_QUESTIONS.md.

      50  - **Decision:** Build the API on ASP.NET Core with .NET 10 (LTS
          ).
      51  - **Reasoning:** .NET 8 reached end of support on 10 Nov 2026, 
          so it no longer receives security patches. A new production sys
          tem should not start on an unsupported runtime. .NET 10 is the 
          current LTS release, supported until Nov 2028. Same framework f
          amily: no architectural change.
      52  - **Alternatives considered:** .NET 8 (out of support, rejected
           on security grounds). .NET 9 (also out of support since 10 Nov
           2026).
      53 -- **Final selection:** Pending approval.                       
      54 -- **Status:** Proposed (OPEN_QUESTIONS H1)                     
      53 +- **Final selection:** Not adopted. The owner chose to stay on 
         +.NET 8 (D-008).                                                
      54 +- **Status:** Rejected (2026-11-18)                            
      55  - **Impact:** SDK installation, CI images, hosting runtime vers
          ion.

      61  - **Decision:** Base both themes on Ink Heather (60%, main), Pu
          tty Grey (30%, secondary) and Canary Flash (10%, accent, `#FFE0
          14`). Proposed reading: dark theme uses Ink Heather backgrounds
           with Putty text and secondary elements; light theme uses warm 
          off-white backgrounds, Putty surfaces and borders, and Ink text
          . Canary is the accent in both themes.
      62  - **Reasoning:** Taken from the reference images. Only Canary h
          as an exact hex value. Ink Heather (≈ `#262A33`) and Putty Grey
           (≈ `#C1BBAF`) were sampled from compressed screenshots.
      63  - **Alternatives considered:** Using image 2's literal swatches
           (its "Ink Heather" swatch shows a light beige, which contradic
          ts image 1).
      64 -- **Final selection:** Pending exact hex values and approval of
         - the light-theme reading.

         +AF` (estimated values accepted), Canary Flash `#FFE014`. Light 
         +and dark mapping as described above.

          lours need a separate semantic set.

      124 +## D-009 — Business rules batch 2 (owner answers, form 3)     
      125 +                                                              
      126 +- **Date:** 2026-11-18                                        
      127 +- **Category:** Business logic                                
      128 +- **Decision:**                                               
      129 +  - **Drivers:** admins manage the driver list; staff choose t
          +he driver at Start (whether drivers can be scanned is still op
          +en: N2b).                                                     
      130 +  - **Hours:** reported both per staff member and per driver. 
      131 +  - **Concurrency:** a staff member can have only one active v
          +ehicle at a time. A driver and a vehicle can each be in only o
          +ne active trip (this follows logically).                      
      132 +  - **Lots for staff:** staff can start vehicles only from the
          +ir assigned lots. At End, staff choose the lot the vehicle ret
          +urned to, which can be any lot.                               
      133 +  - **Reminders:** the first one goes out after a duration the
          + admin sets, then every 30 minutes, and the admin is alerted t
          +oo.                                                           
      134 +  - **Admin manual end:** the admin enters the actual end time
          + and a required reason, and the change is recorded in the audi
          +t log.                                                        
      135 +  - **Capacity:** available spaces are calculated automaticall
          +y, and the admin can set a starting count. A full lot or vehic
          +le type gets a warning but is still allowed. Operating hours a
          +re information only.                                          
      136 +  - **Vehicle statuses:** Available, Active (out of the lot), 
          +Inactive, Under maintenance.                                  
      137 +  - **Transfers:** the admin starts the transfer and staff con
          +firm the vehicle arrived.                                     
      138 +  - **Categories:** each vehicle Type belongs to a Category. C
          +apacity per type uses the Type level (the client's wording "Ve
          +hicle Type-wise Capacity").                                   
      139 +  - **QR codes:** the code carries no readable data. Admins ca
          +n download an image or a printable PDF label, and bulk print. 
          +Staff can type the ID if a scan fails. Admins can also scan. A
          + damaged sticker is reprinted with the same code (my reading, 
          +since "reissue cancels old" wasn't chosen).                   
      140 +  - **Accounts:** the system generates Staff IDs in a fixed pa
          +ttern (pattern still open: K4). More admin accounts can be add
          +ed. The admin resets staff passwords and our team resets the a
          +dmin's. No forced password change at first login.             
      141 +  - **Devices:** staff use their personal phones.             
      142 +  - **Removal:** records are deactivated, never deleted, so hi
          +story is kept.                                                
      143 +  - **Connectivity:** online only.                            
      144 +  - **Tenancy:** a single organisation.                       
      145 +  - **Out of scope for v1:** billing and payments, maintenance
          + management, fuel logs, live vehicle GPS.                     
      146 +  - **Kept from the mockup:** attendance, modify trip, cancel 
          +trip with reason, driver signature, destination location.     
      147 +  - **Dropped from the mockup:** driver QR cards.             
      148 +- **Reasoning:** Owner and client answers given through form 3
          +.                                                             
      149 +- **Alternatives considered:** Listed in `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` f
          +or each question.                                             
      150 +- **Final selection:** As stated.                             
      151 +- **Status:** Approved                                        
      152 +- **Impact:**                                                 
      153 +  - Push notifications (FCM/APNs) and a scheduled background j
          +ob are needed for reminders.                                  
      154 +  - Personal phones plus attendance photos, GPS and signatures
          + mean personal data handling and a privacy policy.            
      155 +  - "Under maintenance" is set by hand; there is no maintenanc
          +e module.                                                     
      156 +  - Follow-up questions are listed under "Round 1d" in `OPEN_Q
          +UESTIONS.md`.

⏺ I've recorded all 31 answers.

  Decision log changes
  - D-004 (palette): approved, using the estimated hex values.
  - D-007 (new): one app for both roles; what each person sees depends on
    whether their account is Admin or Staff. This replaces the original "two
    apps" (D-001).
  - D-008 (new): stay on .NET 8. This replaces my .NET 10 suggestion (D-003, now
    rejected).
  - D-009 (new): all the business rules from your answers.

  CLAUDE.md, PROJECT_OVERVIEW.md and OPEN_QUESTIONS.md are updated to match. The
  dev machine question (H2) is parked until development starts.

  Risks to be aware of (no action needed now)
  - .NET 8: Microsoft stopped security fixes on 10 Nov 2026. Moving to .NET 10
    is usually a small job, so we can revisit it before backend work starts.
  - One app: the original PDF promised the client two apps, so they should be
    told. Admin features will also be installed on staff's personal phones,
    hidden by role, which means the server must check permissions on every
    request.

  Two choices I made from your answers:
  - You didn't select "reissue cancels old QR", so a damaged QR sticker gets
    reprinted with the same code.
  - Capacity per vehicle type uses the Type level, matching the client's wording
    "Vehicle Type-wise Capacity".

  Follow-up form above: it covers the details of the mockup features you kept.
  You can leave the scale and category examples blank. Two answers clashed and
  need sorting out:
  - You chose "pick or scan" for drivers but dropped driver QR cards, so I asked
    how drivers are picked (N2b).
  - Staff can only start trips from their own lots but can return vehicles to
    any lot, so I asked whether they can end a trip at a lot they aren't
    assigned to (C2b).

  Once you submit it, requirements are close to done, and the next step is the
  requirements document (REQUIREMENTS.md, Phase 1).

      166 +  - **Driver fields:** photo, Driver ID, full name, mobile, li

          +a GPS location are mandatory for every entry.                 
      169 +  - **Destinations:** a list the admin manages, structured as 
          +Area → Location.                                              
      170 +  - **Modifying a trip:** swapping the vehicle or driver edits
          + the same trip; no new trip is created.                       
      171 +  - **Cancelling a trip:** the trip ends early and its hours s
          +till count. The vehicle's status is not changed automatically;
          + the admin decides.                                           
      172 +  - **Driver signature:** taken at Start only.                
      173 +  - **Ending at another lot:** staff can end a trip at any lot
          +, including lots they aren't assigned to.                     
      174 +  - **Transfers:** the vehicle stays counted at the origin lot
          + until staff confirm it arrived. A transfer records a driver a
          +nd counts toward operating hours.                             
      175 +- **Reasoning:** Owner answers through form 4.                
      176 +- **Alternatives considered:** Listed in `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` f
          +or each question.

      179 +- **Impact:** Swapping drivers or vehicles inside one trip con
          +flicts with reporting hours per driver and per vehicle (N3, ru
          +nning-hours reports). The proposed fix is to log each swap as

      181 +## D-011 — Staff ID format (proposed)                         
      182 +                                                              
      183 +- **Date:** 2026-11-22                                        
      184 +- **Category:** Business logic                                
      185 +- **Decision:** Staff IDs are `DM` + 2-digit year the account 
          +was created + 5-digit sequence, for example `DM2600042`.      
      186 +  - The prefix is an admin setting.                           
      187 +  - Sequence numbers are never reused, including after a staff
          + member is deactivated.                                       
      188 +  - Login ignores letter case.                                
      189 +- **Reasoning:**                                              
      190 +  - The ID never changes, and it appears in history and audit 
          +records.                                                      
      191 +  - Lots and areas are left out of the ID because staff assign
          +ments change.                                                 
      192 +  - Digits after the prefix avoid O/0 and I/1 mix-ups when typ
          +ing on a phone.                                               
      193 +  - Nine characters is short enough to type at login.         
      194 +- **Alternatives considered:**                                
      195 +  - Encode the lot or area, as the client's example `DM324DU21
          +31` might. Rejected: the ID would go stale when assignments ch
          +ange.                                                         
      196 +  - Add a check digit. Not needed.                            
      197 +- **Final selection:** Pending owner approval (Round 1e, P2). 
      198 +- **Status:** Proposed                                        
      199 +- **Impact:** Login, Staff Management and audit display.

      41  ## Owner answers — 2026-11-18 (form 3)

      43  Recorded as D-004 (palette approved), D-007 (one app), D-008 (.

⏺ I've saved your answers as decision D-010.

  Staff ID format (proposed as D-011, needs your OK in P2): DM + 2-digit year +
  5-digit number, e.g. DM2600042.
  - The DM prefix can be changed by the admin.
  - Numbers are never reused, even after a staff member is deactivated.
  - The ID doesn't include a lot or area. Staff move between lots, and the ID
    has to stay the same everywhere it's recorded.

  A conflict in your answers (P1): swapping a driver or vehicle edits the same
  trip (N6), but hours are reported per driver and per vehicle (N3). If a driver
  is swapped mid-trip, the whole trip's hours would land on the last driver. My
  fix is to keep one trip and record each swap with its time, so hours split
  correctly: driver A 9–11, driver B 11–2.

  Gaps your answers left:
  - P4: a vehicle that breaks down mid-trip may not be at any lot, so lot counts
    could be wrong. Where should it be counted?
  - P5: when do transfer hours start?
  - P6: attendance has no shift times, so "Late" and "Absent" can't be worked
    out. Do you want shift rules?
  - P7: do lunch and tea breaks record a start and an end?
  - P3: are Driver IDs made by the system or entered by the admin?

  All of these are in the form above.

      202 +## D-012 — Business rules batch 4 (form 5 + final questions)  
      203 +                                                              
      204 +- **Date:** 2026-11-22                                        
      205 +- **Category:** Business logic, UX/UI                         
      206 +- **Decision:**                                               
      207 +  - **Hours after a swap:** a trip stays one record, but every
          + driver or vehicle swap is logged as a timed segment. Hours ar
          +e split by segment, per driver and per vehicle.               
      208 +  - **Driver ID:** the driver's existing employee number, ente
          +red by the admin. It must be unique.                          
      209 +  - **Vehicle location after cancel or swap-out:** staff pick 
          +the lot where the vehicle is, or mark it "Not at a lot". In th
          +at case the admin sets its lot and status later.              
      210 +  - **Transfer hours:** start when staff at the origin lot con
          +firm departure, and end when staff confirm arrival. The vehicl
          +e stays counted at the origin lot until arrival (D-010).      
      211 +  - **Attendance rules:** the admin sets the shift start time,
          + working days and a grace period. Late = check-in after shift 
          +start plus grace. Absent = no check-in on a working day.      
      212 +  - **Breaks:** lunch and tea breaks each record a start and a
          +n end.                                                        
      213 +  - **Language:** English in v1. Every layout is built to work
          + right-to-left, so Arabic can be added later without a redesig
          +n.                                                            
      214 +  - **Reports:** viewed on screen with date and other filters.
          + No PDF or Excel export in v1.                                
      215 +- **Reasoning:** Owner answers on 2026-11-22.                 
      216 +- **Alternatives considered:** Listed in `OPEN_QUESTIONS.md`. 
      217 +- **Final selection:** As stated.                             
      218 +- **Status:** Approved                                        
      219 +- **Impact:**                                                 
      220 +  - Trip data needs a segment table.                          
      221 +  - The design system must work in both left-to-right and righ
          +t-to-left layouts (directional icons, start/end spacing).     
      222 +  - No file generation is needed for reports in v1.

⏺ Requirements questions are closed, and I've written the requirements document.
  Before UX starts, it needs your approval of 22 proposals I made where your
  answers left gaps.

  Your last answers (recorded in the decision log):
  - Staff ID format: approved.
  - Driver swap mid-trip: the trip's hours are split at each swap, so each
    driver and vehicle is credited for its own time.
  - Language: English for v1, with every screen built to work right-to-left so
    Arabic can be added later without a redesign.
  - Reports: on-screen with filters only, no PDF or Excel export.

  New files:
  - REQUIREMENTS.md: the full spec. It covers scope, a glossary, who can do what
    (Admin vs Staff), about 70 numbered requirements, business rules, trip and
    transfer flows, performance and security targets, stored data, integrations,
    and open items.
  - DEVELOPMENT_ROADMAP.md: phase status. Requirements is in review, and UX
    design comes next.

  What needs your OK: the 22 proposals (P-01 to P-22, in section 11.1 of the
  requirements). Each requirement shows its source, so you can see which ones
  came from me. The proposals I'd look at most closely:
  - P-02: admins can also swap or cancel any active trip, not just end it.
  - P-07: a vehicle can only be started from the lot it's currently in.
  - P-10: one shift schedule applies to all staff.
  - P-22: only staff start trips; admins monitor and step in when needed.

  Still waiting on (doesn't block design work):
  - How many vehicles, lots, staff and trips per day (needed for technical
    planning).
  - Privacy policy text and how long data is kept.
  - Who owns the Google Maps account.
  - App name and logo.
  - Where it will be hosted.
  - The client needs to be told it's now one app with roles instead of the two
    apps in their PDF.

  Before backend work: .NET 8 no longer gets security updates from Microsoft, so
  we should revisit that choice then.

  Design skills: once UX starts, I'll use ui-ux-designer and ui-ux-pro-max for
  screen structure, user journeys and wireframes, and frontend-design for the
  visual design. frontend-developer is for building screens, so it fits the
  clickable prototypes and later development rather than this phase.

❯ Approve all, start UX

      242 +  - **Navigation:** bottom navigation by role. Staff: Home · T
          +rips · Lots · Attendance. Admin: Dashboard · Live · Manage · R
          +eports.

          +trip runs.

      245 +  - **End trip:** scan the vehicle to confirm it, with the ori
          +gin lot pre-selected as the return lot.

      247 +  - **Live tab (admin):** trips and transfers in one place.

          +tem.

      250 +  - **Attendance:** one next-action button plus a timeline.   
      251 +  - **Dashboard:** "Needs attention" first.                   
      252 +  - **Permissions:** asked just in time, not at first launch. 
      253 +- **Reasoning:**                                              
      254 +  - Staff work in the field and need speed and very few mistak
          +es.

          +s.

⏺ Requirements are approved and logged as decision D-013. The UX draft is ready
  for your review: a structure doc plus 39 greyscale wireframes.

  Wireframes: docs/ux/wireframes.html, open in the Browser pane. There's a
  button at the top to preview the layout right-to-left. I checked every frame
  for clipped content at phone, tablet and desktop widths, and nothing is cut
  off.
  - Common screens: login, QR scanner, the "can't start this vehicle" message,
    profile.
  - Staff: home (before and during a trip), the 5-step start-trip flow, active
    trip, end trip, swap, cancel, my trips, lots list and map, attendance,
    transfer confirmation.
  - Admin: dashboard, live trips, ending a trip as admin, new transfer, manage
    menu, vehicle detail, lot form, reports.
  - Screen states: loading, empty, offline, error, and asking for phone
    permissions.

  Structure doc: docs/ux/UX_ARCHITECTURE.md covers design principles, user
  profiles, navigation, a site map, an inventory of about 60 screens linked to
  the requirements, 9 user journeys with edge cases, screen states, interaction
  patterns, accessibility and right-to-left rules, and wording rules.

  The main UX decisions (logged as proposed in D-014):
  - Staff tabs: Home · Trips · Lots · Attendance. While a trip is running, a
    timer bar stays pinned at the top of every staff screen.
  - Admin tabs: Dashboard · Live · Manage · Reports. The dashboard leads with
    what needs attention (overdue trips, vehicles not at any lot, lots over
    capacity, transfers waiting) instead of decorative number tiles.
  - Start trip takes 5 steps ending in a review screen. End trip takes 4: scan
    the vehicle, confirm the return lot (the starting lot is pre-selected),
    confirm, then see the summary.
  - Ending, cancelling or ending a trip as admin each asks for confirmation and
    says what will happen.

  Not done: these flows haven't been tested with real staff. A quick check with
  2–3 staff and one admin before the high-fidelity designs would be cheap and
  worth it.

  Design skills: I used ui-ux-designer and ui-ux-pro-max here. ui-ux-pro-max's
  suggested design system (a blue palette and a landing-page layout) doesn't fit
  this product, so I ignored it; your approved palette stands. frontend-design
  comes in with the design-system phase.

  I can also publish the wireframes as a private shareable page if you want to
  show the client.

⏺ I've built a themed preview: your palette applied to 14 key screens, shown in
  light and dark side by side. It's open in the Browser pane, with buttons at
  the top to switch between Light, Dark and Side by side, and to flip the layout
  to right-to-left.

  Preview: docs/design/ui-preview.html
  - Foundations: colour swatches for both themes, status labels, the type scale
    and basic components.
  - Staff: Login, Home (before and during a trip), choose vehicle, choose
    driver, review, overdue trip, return lot, end confirmation, trip summary,
    attendance, lots.
  - Admin: Dashboard, Live trips.

  Design direction, "Fleet Signage": Ink Heather and Putty Grey make up most of
  the interface. Canary yellow works like hi-vis road signage and appears only
  for what's live right now or the main action.
  - Signature element: a canary strip with the vehicle plate and a running
    timer, pinned to the top of every staff screen while a trip runs. It turns
    solid red with a warning icon and the word "Overdue" when the trip runs
    late.
  - Plate badges: registration numbers are drawn as small licence plates, so
    vehicles are easy to tell apart at a glance.
  - Fonts:
    - Readex Pro for headings.
    - IBM Plex Sans for body text.
    - IBM Plex Mono for timers and IDs, so ticking digits don't jump around.

    Readex Pro and Plex Sans both have Arabic versions, which keeps the later
    Arabic version straightforward.
  - Status colours: muted, always shown with an icon and a word. Warnings are
    orange, so they're never confused with the canary accent.

  Checks:
  - Every text and background colour pairing meets accessibility contrast
    standards. I measured each one; the ratios are listed in the design doc.
  - No screen clips its content in either theme or in right-to-left.
  - I found and fixed one CSS clash that was adding extra space above some
    buttons.
  - Not done: testing on real phones or with real staff.

  Details: docs/design/DESIGN_SYSTEM.md has the full spec: colour tokens with
  contrast ratios, type scale, spacing, components, motion, right-to-left rules,
  and how it maps to Flutter.

  Decision log:
  - D-014 (the layout and navigation from the wireframes) and D-015 (this design
    system) are both waiting for your approval.
  - D-016 records that you chose to review the two together on themed screens.

  Once both are approved, the next step is designing all the remaining screens
  (about 60) in both themes.

       98 -**Why these fonts:**                                          
       99 -- **Readex Pro** is geometric and wide, close to the reference
          -'s signage feel, and has an **Arabic** design built in.       
      100 -- **IBM Plex Sans** is a sober UI text face, with **IBM Plex S
          -ans Arabic** for later.                                       
      101 -- **IBM Plex Mono** gives timers and IDs fixed-width digits, s
          -o they never jitter as they tick.                             
      102 -- All three are open source (OFL). They're bundled as app asse
          -ts, never fetched at runtime.                                 
       71 +| Role | Size / weight | Use |                                
       72 +|---|---|---|                                                 
       73 +| Timer | 44 / 800, −1.5 tracking | Active trip, summary |    
       74 +| Screen title | 28 / 800 | Tab roots |                       
       75 +| Section / sheet title | 20 / 800 | Steps, sheets |          
       76 +| Card title | 16.5 / 700 | Rows, cards |                     
       77 +| Body | 14.5–15 / 400–500 | Text, inputs |                   
       78 +| Caption | 12–13 / 500–600 | Meta, labels |                  
       79  
      104 -**Rules**                                                     
      105 -- Sentence case everywhere.                                   
      106 -- Body text is at least 15 and captions at least 12.          
      107 -- Supports system text scaling up to 200%: rows grow taller in
          -stead of cutting text off.                                    
       80 +**Arabic (later, D-012):** pair with IBM Plex Sans Arabic or N
          +oto Kufi Arabic. Plus Jakarta Sans has no Arabic script.      
       81  
      109 -## 4. Space, shape, elevation                                 
       82 +## 4. Shape, space, elevation, icons                          
       83  
      111 -- **Spacing (4-pt):** `4 · 8 · 12 · 16 · 20 · 24 · 32 · 40`. S
          -creen gutter 16; gap between cards 12; space inside cards 16. 
      112 -- **Radius:** `xs 6` (plates, badges) · `sm 10` (inputs, chips
          -) · `md 14` (cards, buttons) · `lg 22` (sheets) · `pill`.     
       84 +- **Spacing:** 4-pt grid. Screen gutter 20.

          +ts 16, chips 12, tiles 14, sheets 30.                         
       86  - **Elevation:**
      114 -  - Light: a 1 px `border` plus a soft shadow `0 1px 2px rgba(
          -31,34,41,.06)`. Sheets get `0 -8px 24px rgba(31,34,41,.16)`.  
      115 -  - Dark: no shadows; depth comes from `bg → surface → surface
          --2`.

          -left.

      103 +## 6. Flutter mapping (Phase 8)                               
      104  
      145 -- 150–250 ms, ease-out.                                       
      146 -- Only three things move:                                     
      147 -  - the live strip's dot (a slow 2 s pulse)

          -eft).                                                         
      150 -- No decorative motion. Reduced-motion settings are respected.
      151 -

          -e start edge; step bars fill from the start.                  
      156 -- Plates, timers and IDs stay left-to-right inside right-to-le
          -ft text.                                                      
      157 -

      92  ## 5. Core business rules (quick reference)
      93  
      94  - **Trip:** Start = the vehicle leaves the lot; End = it return
          s (to any lot). Server time only. One active vehicle per staff 
          member, and one active trip per driver and per vehicle.

⏺ I redid the design with a new look, built it as a dummy app, and turned
  screenshots of it into a PDF. The PDF is attached above, and all project
  knowledge is now saved in one file.

  The new look, "Emerald Ops" (I chose the colours, as you allowed):
  - Emerald green is the main colour: buttons, the active tab, and the big
    highlighted card on each screen.
  - Amber is only for live things: the trip timer bar at the top, vehicles
    currently out, breaks in progress.
  - Dark mode uses a deep night background with bright mint green.
  - What makes it distinctive:
    - an amber bar with the plate number and a running timer stays at the top
      while a vehicle is out, and turns red when the trip is overdue
    - registration numbers are drawn as UAE-style plates
    - simple drawings of garbage trucks, cars and ambulances
    - a map, charts, and a raised Scan button in the centre of the bottom menu
  - Font: Plus Jakarta Sans.
  - Every text colour meets accessibility contrast standards (I calculated each
    pair).

  The PDF: UI_Mockups_v1.pdf (docs/design/mockups/UI_Mockups_v1.pdf), 15 pages.
  - A cover page and a design-language page (colours, type, status labels).
  - 24 screens in light mode: the staff flow from sign-in and scanning through
    starting a trip, an overdue trip, ending, cancelling, trip history, parking
    lots and attendance; and the admin dashboard, live trips, ending a trip as
    admin, new transfer, manage, vehicle, lot and report screens.
  - The same 24 screens in dark mode.
  - The last page shows two alternative colour schemes, Ocean (blue + orange)
    and Midnight Lime, in case you prefer one. Switching takes one setting.

  I looked through the PDF pages myself and fixed three problems: grey boxes
  behind the phones, a broken Scan button, and buttons sitting too close to the
  bottom edge. The 52 full-resolution screenshots are in
  docs/design/mockups/png/.

  How it's built: the dummy app is in prototype/ui-mock/, and python3 -I 
  tools/render_mockups.py re-renders all the screens and rebuilds the PDF in
  about 2 minutes. That command needs Chrome and an internet connection for the
  fonts. A real Flutter dummy app wasn't possible yet, because Flutter, Xcode
  and the Android tools aren't installed on this Mac.

  Project knowledge: docs/KNOWLEDGE_BASE.md now holds everything about the
  project:
  - the product and client context, and the source documents
  - a summary of every decision (now up to D-019) and the core business rules
  - your working preferences
  - the state of this Mac and the tools, and how to re-render
  - open items and external links

  CLAUDE.md points to it as the first thing to read in a new session. I also
  saved two notes for future sessions: you prefer answering questions through
  option forms, and you want realistic visual mock-ups as a PDF.

  Waiting on your decision:
  - D-017: the new colours.
  - D-019: the centre Scan button.
  - D-014: the screen layout and navigation.

  I've marked the old palette as replaced and the previous "Fleet Signage"
  design as rejected. About 35 screens still need full designs: forms, settings,
  notifications, the swap flow, attendance capture, and empty, error and
  offline states.

      72 +def sync_icon_subset():                                        
      73 +    """Keep the Material Symbols icon_names subset in screens.h
         +tml equal to the icons actually used."""                       
      74 +    page = MOCK / "screens.html"                               
      75 +    src = page.read_text()                                     
      76 +    used = set(re.findall(r'class="ms[^"]*"[^>]*>([a-z0-9_]+)<'
         +, src))                                                        
      77 +    used |= set(re.findall(r"'([a-z0-9_]+)'", (MOCK / "app.js")
         +.read_text())) & {                                             
      78 +        "home", "route", "local_parking", "person", "space_dash
         +board", "sensors", "tune", "bar_chart"}                        
      79 +    used |= {"qr_code_scanner", "signal_cellular_alt", "wifi", 
         +"battery_full"}  # injected by app.js                          
      80 +    names = ",".join(sorted(used))                             
      81 +    new = re.sub(r"icon_names=[a-z0-9_,]+", f"icon_names={names
         +}", src)                                                       
      82 +    if new != src:                                             
      83 +        page.write_text(new)                                   
      84 +        print(f"icon subset updated ({len(used)} icons)")      
      85 +                                                               
      86 +                                                               
      87  def file_url(path: Path, query: str = "") -> str:
      88      return "file://" + quote(str(path)) + (("?" + query) if que
          ry else "")

      354    - **Admin tab bar:** Dashboard · Live · Scan · Manage · Repo
           rts. Admins scan to open a vehicle.
      355  - **Reasoning:** Scanning is the most frequent staff action. T
           his puts it one thumb-reach away on every screen. It refines U
           X-01 (D-014).
      356  - **Alternatives considered:** A scan button only on Home and 
           in the top bar (wireframe v0.1).

      359  - **Impact:** `UX_ARCHITECTURE.md` §3 and the Phase 8 navigati
           on shell.
      360 +                                                              
      361 +## D-020 — The staff member is the driver                     
      362 +                                                              
      363 +- **Date:** 2026-11-23                                        
      364 +- **Category:** Business logic                                
      365 +- **Decision:** There is no separate driver; the staff member 
          +who starts a trip drives it.                                  
      366 +  - **Removed:** driver records, driver management, the driver
          + picker and Driver ID (DRV-01–03; D-009 driver list; D-010 dri
          +ver fields; D-012 Driver ID).                                 
      367 +  - **Staff profile:** gains the driving licence number.      
      368 +  - **Start flow:** vehicle (scan) → destination → **staff sig
          +nature** → review. The signature step is kept: the staff membe
          +r signs to confirm taking the vehicle.                        
      369 +  - **Swap:** change the vehicle, **or hand the trip over to a
          +nother staff member**. A hand-over starts a new segment and th
          +e receiving staff member must have no active trip. Hours are s
          +plit by segment per staff member and per vehicle (replaces P-1
          +1).                                                           
      370 +  - **Transfers:** no driver is chosen upfront. Any staff memb
          +er of the origin lot can confirm departure, and whoever confir
          +ms it drives the vehicle. Arrival is confirmed by any staff me
          +mber.                                                         
      371 +  - **Trip records:** the client's "Driver Name" field equals 
          +the staff member, so the summary shows "Driver (staff)".      
      372 +- **Reasoning:** Owner instruction ("driver is staff") plus an
          +swers on 2026-11-23.                                          
      373 +- **Alternatives considered:**

       97 +> **2026-11-23 overrides (D-020 to D-022):**

           f member, and one active trip per driver and per vehicle.

      108    1. origin lot (assigned lots only)

⏺ I updated the PDF (attached above): UI_Mockups_v1.pdf
  (docs/design/mockups/UI_Mockups_v1.pdf), 15 pages, now 25 screens in light and
  dark. I checked every page.

  Staff menu:
  - The bottom menu is now Home · Trips · Scan (centre button) · Lots · Profile.
  - Attendance is no longer a tab. It opens from the attendance card on Home and
    from Profile.
  - The new Profile screen has your details, driving licence, assigned lots,
    attendance, theme, change password and log out.

  Driver is the staff member:
  - The driver picker is gone. Starting a trip is now: scan vehicle →
    destination → your signature → review.
  - The staff member still signs, as you chose.
  - The trip summary, review screen and My trips now show the staff member as
    the driver.
  - Swap now offers two options: change the vehicle, or hand the trip over to
    another staff member. The overdue screen shows an example hand-over.
  - New transfer no longer has a driver field. Any staff member at the starting
    lot confirms departure, and whoever confirms drives the vehicle.
  - In Manage, Drivers is removed and the licence now sits under Staff.

  Admin:
  - The bottom menu is Dashboard · Live · Manage · Reports, with no scan button.
  - The "end trip as admin" screen is replaced by a view-only trip detail with a
    reminder log and the note "Monitoring only".

  Documents: the changes are logged as decisions D-020 to D-022, which replace
  the earlier ones about drivers, admin ending, and the scan button in both
  menus. REQUIREMENTS.md is now v1.1, with a changes section at the top. The UX
  doc, open questions and knowledge base are updated to match.

  One gap this creates: since admins can no longer end trips, a trip can't be
  closed if the staff member can't end it themselves, for example a lost phone
  or someone who has left the job. The vehicle would stay "on trip" and its
  hours would keep counting. I've logged it as open item OI-10 and it needs your
  call before technical planning.

      108  - **Trip:** Start = the vehicle leaves the lot; End = it retur
           ns (to any lot). Server time only. One active vehicle per staf
           f member, and one active trip per driver and per vehicle.

⏺ Attendance is now removed from the whole PDF (attached above).
  UI_Mockups_v1.pdf (docs/design/mockups/UI_Mockups_v1.pdf) is rebuilt with 24
  screens in light and dark, and I checked every page.

  What changed on the screens:
  - Attendance page: deleted. The "Staff · Everyday" page now shows Lots,
    Profile and Sign in.
  - Home (no trip): the attendance card is replaced by a "Last trip" card.
  - Home (trip running): the attendance card is replaced by a "Reminder at
    12:12" note.
  - Profile: the attendance row is replaced by "My trips this month · 42 trips ·
    86 h".
  - Admin dashboard: "Staff on duty" (which came from attendance) is replaced by
    "Free spaces".
  - The screen files and the PDF captions have no attendance mentions left.

  Docs: I've recorded this as decision D-023: attendance is removed from the
  app, not just hidden from the menu. That also drops check-in, breaks, selfie
  photo, GPS, shift rules, Late/Absent and the attendance report.
  REQUIREMENTS.md §0, the UX doc, the open questions and the knowledge base are
  updated.

  Side effect: the app no longer needs location permission or stores selfies, so
  there's less personal data to cover in the privacy policy.

  Still open (OI-10): since admins can't end trips, a trip can't be closed if
  the staff member is unable to end it (for example a lost phone). This needs
  your call before technical planning.
